# MACCREv2 Feature Requests

## Entry Doctrine
All entries in this document must be thoroughly explained and adhere to the following header format:
- **Feature Name:** [Name]
- **Abstract:** [Short semantic abstract]
- **Date/Time Entered:** [ISO-8601 or similar timestamp]
- **Status:** [Fulfilled / Unfulfilled]

### Feature Name: Generative Recruitment Engine (Prompt Engineer Evolution)
**Abstract:** Evolve the Prompt Engineer into a silent, passive context-monitor that dynamically generates and recruits new specialized agents based on conversation context.
**Date/Time Entered:** 2026-06-22T22:52:42-04:00
**Status:** Unfulfilled

**Description:**
Save the Prompt Engineer to the agent roster and equip it with tools allowing it to save agents directly to the roster when requested. Furthermore, add it as a special node that passively monitors the total context window during sessions. It will silently create new, specialized agents that it determines would be useful based on the conversation context, saving them to a "recruitment roster" for analysis, testing, and potential promotion to the Global agent roster. This transforms the Prompt Engineer into a Generative Recruitment Engine as part of the GAN/GaN (Generative Adversarial/assistive Networks) structure.

### 📋 SWARM BRIEFING: BLIND CODE INSPECTION (GAN)
**To: The Prompt Engineer**
**Objective:** Generate a highly specialized, 5-agent swarm dedicated to adversarial "blind" code inspection. These agents should be designed to aggressively audit code segments without assuming project-wide context, identifying vulnerabilities, race conditions, and optimization failures before they reach production.

**Requested Roster (5 Agents):**

**1. Architectural Auditor (The Purist)**
*   **Role:** Analyzes structural integrity and design patterns.
*   **Function:** Reviews code strictly for adherence to the Sovereign Edge Doctrine (e.g., no external networking wrappers, strict relative pathing, interface abstraction). It should aggressively flag hardcoded paths, missing explicit types, and monolithic functions.

**2. Security & Vulnerability Scout (The Breaker)**
*   **Role:** Penetration testing and injection prevention.
*   **Function:** Specifically hunts for unprotected I/O operations, environment variable poisoning risks, unsafe deserialization, and missing context managers (`with` blocks) that could lead to zombie processes or database locks.

**3. Performance & Memory Profiler (The Optimizer)**
*   **Role:** Resource management and runtime efficiency.
*   **Function:** Inspects data structures, loop constraints, and SQLite transaction bounds. It should flag expensive full-table scans, memory-heavy array loading, and missing generator paradigms. 

**4. Logical Consistency Judge (The "Blind" Critic)**
*   **Role:** Abstract logic stress-testing.
*   **Function:** This is the core adversarial agent. It must assume absolutely *no* outside context. Given a logic flow, it actively attempts to conceptualize race conditions, off-by-one errors, and state-desyncs. It does not care what the code is *supposed* to do, only how it can technically fail.

**5. Synthesis Director (The Arbiter)**
*   **Role:** Executive summary and remediation formatting.
*   **Function:** Ingests the findings from the previous 4 agents. Its job is to filter out semantic noise and false positives, consolidating the genuine vulnerabilities into a strictly formatted, actionable Markdown ledger.

### Feature Name: Session Manager Dashboard
**Abstract:** Introduce a dedicated UI within the TUI to view, rename, and manually canonize historical job sessions.
**Date/Time Entered:** 2026-06-23T21:13:00-04:00
**Status:** Unfulfilled

**Description:**
The TUI needs a "Session Manager" interface (perhaps accessible from the project selection or an admin dashboard). This UI should list all job sessions from `project_registry.db`, allowing users to rename sessions (giving them a semantic label instead of just a timestamp), view the final artifact outputs or unified ledgers, and trigger a manual "Canonization" pipeline to elevate their thought pins into the project's permanent semantic memory. The manager should also display whether a session has already been canonized to avoid duplicate entries.

### Linear Flow & Special Nodes Enhancements
- **Branching & Aggregation Nodes**: DET_FAN_OUT to dynamically spawn N parallel sub-tasks/branches. DET_SYNTHESIZE (or DET_AGGREGATE) to wait for multiple prerequisite branches to finish and merge their payloads.
- **Filtering & Parsing Nodes**: DET_FILTER_IN to conditionally pass payload forward based on regex/keyword matching. DET_EXTRACT to isolate specific text using regex capture groups.
- **Dynamic Routing**: DET_DYNAMIC_ROUTER to route based on payload contents rather than static loop counters.
- **Webhooks**: DET_WEBHOOK for lightweight HTTP event triggers.
- **Local Edge LLM Sync Node (S25 Ultra Integration)**: A node that drops a payload into a specific Google Drive folder, paired with a watchdog node that polls the folder for a response from an edge local LLM (polling every few minutes, scaling to every 5 seconds when active, then scaling back down after 5 minutes of inactivity). Useful for S25 Ultra or status updates on edge nodes.

### Feature Name: Multi-Tier Grounding Options & Hybrid Exclusionary Search
**Abstract:** Add "Grounding with Local Memory" and "Grounding with Brave LLM Search" toggles to the Agent Builder, with complex exclusionary logic when multiple groundings are active.
**Date/Time Entered:** 2026-06-24T17:30:00-04:00
**Status:** Unfulfilled

**Description:**
Add "Grounding with Local Memory" and "Grounding with Brave LLM Search" toggles to the Agent Builder right below Grounding with Google Search. 
- **Hybrid Exclusionary Search**: If Google and Brave are both turned on at the same time, it automatically triggers logic to wait for the model to do its Google searches, the agent thinks about the Google search results and selects sources, and then uses what it's learned to do a Brave search that explicitly ignores results already discovered by the Google search, before continuing. If both are turned on, the agent *always* uses this hybrid exclusionary search logic. 
- **Tri-Grounding Logic**: If all 3 Groundings (Google, Brave, Local Memory) are turned on, the agent's system prompt must be dynamically injected with context explaining *why* it would search its project memory and that it has the capability to do so. It must be explicitly instructed that project memory is only a source of truth if referencing the project and project-related contexts (to avoid hallucinating local data as global internet facts).

### Feature Name: TUI State Container & Asynchronous Rendering Architecture
**Abstract:** Decouple the TUI from direct synchronous SQLite queries by implementing an event-driven, in-memory State Container and leveraging Textual's Worker API to eliminate main-thread blocking.
**Date/Time Entered:** 2026-06-30T17:58:20-04:00
**Status:** Unfulfilled

**Description:**
As the Sovereign Datacenter expands and Nexus workflows grow in complexity, the Textual UI can experience rendering latency (frame stutter, slow modal mounts). This is primarily caused by synchronous blocking operations on the main event loop (e.g., massive JSON parsing, deep SQLite queries, or mounting thousands of DOM nodes at once). 
**Proposed Enhancements:**
1. **Polled Database State Objects (State Container):** Build an in-memory buffer (Python dictionaries) that loads SQLite tables in the background. The TUI reads exclusively from this instantaneous memory buffer. The buffer updates via background polling or event triggers when an agent modifies the database.
2. **Textual Worker API (`@work(thread=True)`):** Push heavy processing off the main asyncio thread. Modals should mount instantly with loaders, dispatch a background worker to query data, and populate upon receiving a callback.
3. **DOM Optimization:** Implement lazy-loading or pagination for massive files (like reading the `unified_session_ledger.md`) to prevent the Textual engine from recalculating layout syntax highlighting for thousands of nodes on a single tick.

***

### Feature Name: Nexus Copilot Sovereign Sandbox Enhancement (antigravity-preview-05-2026)
**Abstract:** Elevate Nexus Copilot from conversational topology creation to full sovereign sandbox execution using antigravity-preview-05-2026 capabilities with the local .venv as a provisioned sandbox environment.
**Date/Time Entered:** 2026-07-11T11:56:00-04:00
**Status:** Unfulfilled

**Description:**
The Nexus Copilot is currently primed for topology creation and has demonstrated the ability to create MacroNodes from conversational context, as well as possessing nascent capabilities for repairing broken topologies. This feature request proposes a significant capability uplift:

1. **Sandbox Provisioning:** Leverage `antigravity-preview-05-2026` and its ability to use the local `.venv` as a provisioned sandbox environment for Nexus Copilot. This will enable the Copilot to execute code, validate topologies, and test configurations locally — elevating its reasoning and capability to match the Primary Engineering Agent's level.

2. **Sovereign REST Integration:** This MUST NOT use the Antigravity SDK. Instead, use and augment the existing sovereign REST capabilities (`maccre_core._net.gemini_client` / `UniversalRouter`) to maintain the system's zero-dependency edge compatibility and data sovereignty.

3. **Enhanced Capabilities:**
   - **Topology Validation:** Execute `build_from_template()` in the sandbox to validate MacroNode configurations before saving.
   - **Topology Repair:** Detect broken topologies (missing agents, invalid Wait_For references, orphaned nodes) and propose/apply fixes using sandbox execution.
   - **MacroNode Generation:** Convert natural language descriptions into complete MacroNode configurations with validated topology graphs.
   - **Control Node Composition:** Assemble custom topologies from the Control Node (DET) primitive library with sandbox-validated wiring.

4. **Assessment Required:**
   - Audit current Copilot topology creation capabilities and success rate.
   - Map existing REST client capabilities vs. what antigravity-preview-05-2026 requires.
   - Identify integration points between the Copilot's tool registry and the sandbox environment.
   - Ensure all sandbox operations remain within the Sovereign Physical Laws (no external network dependencies, all paths relative via `get_maccre_root()`).

***

### Feature Name: Deterministic CI/CD Plan Reasoning Engine & Reasoning Confidence Score (RCS)
**Abstract:** A deterministic CI/CD reasoning tool (`omni evaluate-plan`) that mathematically evaluates proposed implementation plans across 5 Oracle vectors and appends a Reasoning Confidence Score (RCS) before review.
**Date/Time Entered:** 2026-07-28T20:04:36-04:00
**Status:** Unfulfilled

**Description:**
As the 5 Specialist Oracles (NetAndClient, OrchestrationAndEngine, TUIAndInterface, ToolsAndRAG, StateAndSovereignty) provide deep, granular codebase auditing, tracking all domain-specific nuances creates exponential cognitive load ($M_{\text{load}} \propto N_{\text{domains}} \times \text{Granularity} \times \text{Dependencies}$). 

This feature introduces an automated reasoning gatekeeper integrated into the `omni` CI/CD toolchain (`omni evaluate-plan`). The tool evaluates proposed plans prior to user presentation and calculates a composite **Reasoning Confidence Score (RCS)**:

$$RCS = w_1 \cdot C_{\text{oracle}} + w_2 \cdot S_{\text{contract}} + w_3 \cdot G_{\text{completeness}} + w_4 \cdot E_{\text{economics}} - w_5 \cdot R_{\text{risk}}$$

**Core Vectors Evaluated:**
1. **Inter-Oracle Consensus ($C_{\text{oracle}}$)**: Measures graph contract alignment across all 5 Oracle domain boundaries.
2. **Static AST & Contract Soundness ($S_{\text{contract}}$)**: Verifies symbol preservation, type safety, and SQLite database schema constraints (e.g. preventing `UNIQUE(job_id, current_node)` collisions).
3. **Intent Delta & Requirement Completeness ($G_{\text{completeness}}$)**: Evaluates plan evolution across iterative versions (v1 → v2 → v3), ensuring core requirements and conceptual refinements discovered during early drafts are not silently dropped as plans progress.
4. **Edge Economics & Hardware Constraints ($E_{\text{economics}}$)**: Probes API rate limits (RPM/TPM), local model VRAM bounds, and SQLite WAL write lock limits.
5. **Unknown-Unknown Risk Penalty ($R_{\text{risk}}$)**: Penalizes un-sandboxed I/O, un-synchronized thread singletons, and unhedged memory zeroing (`ctypes.memset`).

**Ideological Evolution Rationale:**
Logged as a functional synthesis of ideology and evolution—retaining executive human oversight while building deterministic automation rails to automate oversight reliability without ceding ultimate architectural authority.

# CONSOLIDATION — 2026-08-31

`SessionManager_FileCabinet-SovImporter-Integration.md` was merged into this
document on 2026-08-31. That file is now a redirect stub. **This document is the
single formal register of user feature requests and verified-but-unscheduled
defects.** A copy is kept at
`.kiro_artifacts/FeatureRequests.md` for tracking continuity.

### Doctrine addendum

The original Entry Doctrine stands. Two clarifications for entries added from
2026-08-31 onward:

- **Defect-class entries** carry a `**Verified:**` line stating how the defect was
  observed. An entry marked *user-reported* has **not** been reproduced in code;
  its stated cause is a lead to check, not a finding. This distinction matters —
  three consecutive engine defects this phase had plausible-but-wrong first
  hypotheses.
- **Status** may additionally read *Deferred (needs decision)* where the fix is
  understood but changes shared behaviour and wants operator sign-off, or
  *Deferred (needs prerequisite)* where another item must land first.

Entries below are **not** blocking the Phase 6.13 scatter/tether work in progress.

***

### Feature Name: Session Manager — "Name MacroNode" modal is a dead end
**Abstract:** Naming a completed session to save it as a MacroNode opens a modal with no Save and no Cancel button, trapping the operator; the modal also never explains what saving a session as a MacroNode actually captures.
**Date/Time Entered:** 2026-08-30T00:00:00-04:00
**Status:** Unfulfilled
**Verified:** User-reported. **Not reproduced in code.** The cause hypothesis below is a lead.

**Description:**
Two separate fixes are needed.

**(a) The modal needs its buttons.** Worth checking against the same class of bug
already scheduled as Phase 6.13 Task B4, where `NodeConfigModal` required
`container.refresh(layout=True)` after dynamically mounting widgets or the mounted
controls never appeared. If this modal composes its buttons dynamically it is
plausibly the identical defect. Determine whether the buttons are *absent from the
compose tree* or merely *unrendered* — those are different bugs with the same
appearance.

**(b) The modal must explain what it does.** "Save this completed session as a
MacroNode" is not self-evident. The operator currently cannot tell:
- what is captured — topology only, or topology plus agent assignments, model
  overrides, tool grants and custom instructions?
- whether payloads and ledgers come along, or only the wiring
- whether it lands in the **project** MacroNode store or **GLOBAL**. `FlowRunner`
  reads project first then falls back to GLOBAL, so this choice decides whether the
  artifact is visible from other projects.
- what happens on a name collision: overwrite, error, or silent duplicate?
- whether control nodes are captured as authored or as resolved primitives.
  `resolve_primitive_node_id` maps authoring aliases onto the primitive that
  implements them, so a save/reuse round trip may not be identity.

The last two matter most. A silent overwrite is data loss, and a non-identity round
trip means "save and reuse" does not reproduce the flow just run.

**Related:** the existing *Session Manager Dashboard* entry (2026-06-23) covers the
broader UI. This entry is the specific blocking defect within it.

***

### Feature Name: Session Manager — "Name Session" input does not accept entry
**Abstract:** The Name Session field in the main TUI, beside the Clear Flow button, does not take input and may not be wired at all.
**Date/Time Entered:** 2026-08-30T00:00:00-04:00
**Status:** Unfulfilled
**Verified:** User-reported. **Not reproduced in code.**

**Description:**
Sessions remain addressable by generated job id, so nothing is lost — but every
session stays anonymous, which makes the File Cabinet / Sovereign Importer work
markedly less useful. A cabinet of unnamed sessions is a cabinet of
`job_20260831-041428-6goe`.

Leads to check:
- is the widget actually an `Input`, or a `Static`/`Label` styled to look like one?
- is it disabled, or covered by another widget in the layout?
- does an `on_input_submitted` / `on_input_changed` handler exist, and does anything
  read the value?
- **is there a write path at all?** `create_session` and `update_session_status`
  exist; a session *name* may simply have no storage.

That last point decides the size of the job. With no column this is not a wiring fix
but a small schema change plus migration, in the shape of Phase 6.13 Task A1's
`locked_at` addition.

***

### Feature Name: Session Manager / File Cabinet alignment for Sovereign Importer
**Abstract:** Establish, user-test and document the Session Manager and File Cabinet surfaces so the Sovereign Importer project's coding agent can integrate against a stable, evidenced contract.
**Date/Time Entered:** 2026-08-30T00:00:00-04:00
**Status:** Deferred (needs prerequisite — the two defects above)
**Verified:** N/A — scoping item, not a defect.

**Description:**
Both defects above sit in the Session Manager's own surface, and this integration
consumes that surface. Handing an integration partner an API whose naming path is
broken invites them to design around the breakage, which then has to be undone.

**(a) Research the existing code.** Establish what exists rather than what is
assumed: what the Session Manager reads and writes and through which broker methods;
how the File Cabinet enumerates and addresses artifacts; the on-disk layout
(`03_Agent_Ledgers/<job_id>/`, `04_Code_Artifacts/<job_id>/`) and which paths are
contractual versus incidental; where the unified session ledger fits given it is
both an output *and* the next node's input payload; whether session identity is
stable enough to serve as a foreign key for an external importer.

**(b) User-test for a baseline.** Same discipline as Phase 6.13's UT-0, and for the
same reason: a 665-test suite was green while four consecutive scatter defects
sat in the seam between the authoring UI and the engine. A Session Manager / File
Cabinet integration is *entirely* such a seam. Script the walkthrough, run it by
hand, record what actually happens including the failure modes, before writing any
integration document.

**(c) Produce an integration report** stating, with observed evidence rather than
intent: the stable addressing scheme for a session and its artifacts; which fields
are guaranteed versus best-effort; the lifecycle — when a session becomes
importable, and whether `cancelled` / `failed` / stalled sessions are importable at
all; known gaps explicitly, including anything from the two defects still
outstanding; and the round-trip question from (a) — does export/import reproduce a
*runnable* flow?

**Critical caveat on session status.** Phase 6.13 Task A5 added a terminal
condition: a session can now be recorded `failed` because a step **stalled** (tasks
left locked with no worker alive). An importer enumerating by status must know that
`failed` now covers that case, and that a stalled session's artifacts are
**incomplete by definition** — at least one node was claimed and never ran.
Importing one as whole would propagate a hole. See also the `timeout` entry below:
until that is settled, `completed` is not proof that every step finished.

***

### Feature Name: A timed-out step does not stop the flow
**Abstract:** `FlowRunner` breaks its step loop on `cancelled` and `stalled` but not on `timeout`; a timed-out step lets the flow continue and the session is still recorded `completed`.
**Date/Time Entered:** 2026-08-30T00:00:00-04:00
**Status:** Deferred (needs decision)
**Verified:** Read directly in `maccre_core/orchestration/flow_engine.py` — both `execute_flow` and `resume_flow` test only `pool_status == "cancelled"` and (since Task A5) `"stalled"`.

**Description:**
This is the same silent-success shape that Task A5 removed for stalls: work does not
finish, and the session reports success anyway. It was deliberately **not** bundled
into A5 because changing timeout semantics is a behaviour change on a shared path and
a timeout is more ambiguous than a stall — it may legitimately mean "needs a longer
budget" rather than "work was lost".

Recommendation: a timed-out step should stop the flow and mark the session `failed`,
matching the stall path. Needs operator sign-off because it will fail flows that
currently report success.

Consequence while unresolved: **`completed` is weaker than it looks**, and anything
enumerating sessions by status — including the Sovereign Importer and any future
spatial map — must not treat it as proof that every step ran.

***

### Feature Name: `omni qa` Pyright blind spot is hiding live type errors
**Abstract:** `omni.py` passes `pyright maccre_core`, and an explicit path overrides `pyrightconfig.json`'s include list — so `maccre_mcp.py` and `maccre.py` have never been type-checked by the gate, and `maccre_mcp.py` currently has 6 errors.
**Date/Time Entered:** 2026-08-30T00:00:00-04:00
**Status:** Deferred (needs decision)
**Verified:** Reproduced. Running Pyright config-driven (no path argument) reports **6 errors, 1 warning**.

**Description:**
`pyrightconfig.json` declares `"include": ["maccre_core", "maccre_mcp.py", "maccre.py"]`.
`omni.py` narrows it:

    pyright_target = "maccre_core" if os.path.isdir("maccre_core") else target

Errors currently hidden:

    maccre_mcp.py:105 - "get_native_credential" is unknown import symbol
    maccre_mcp.py:658 - "get_native_credential" is unknown import symbol
    maccre_mcp.py:109 - Argument of type "str" cannot be assigned to parameter
                        "key_provider" of type "() -> (str | None)" in get_sentinel
    maccre_mcp.py:663 - same, in get_registry

The last two are not stylistic. `get_registry` / `get_sentinel` expect a *callable*
provider and invoke it; a bare `str` fails at call time. The first two suggest an
import that may not resolve at all.

**Not changed yet, deliberately** — flipping the scope turns `omni qa` red
immediately, and it was found mid-way through a live test cycle. Sequence: fix
`maccre_mcp.py`, then drop the explicit path so `pyrightconfig.json` is
authoritative, then confirm green. Recorded so the gate's *true* coverage is not
mistaken for its *documented* coverage.

***

### Feature Name: `omni` telemetry logger with 30-day semantic compaction
**Abstract:** Give omni a dedicated telemetry log of its own actions that self-compacts every 30 days into an ultra-lightweight abbreviated heuristic, accompanied by an in-file instruction set letting any AI or human semantically re-expand the abbreviations back into full log entries on demand.
**Date/Time Entered:** 2026-08-31T00:00:00-04:00
**Status:** Unfulfilled
**Verified:** N/A — new capability. Explicitly flagged by the operator as **not a priority**.

**Description:**
Goal: a rarely-to-never-read, near-zero-footprint archive that remains losslessly
*interpretable* rather than losslessly *stored*.

**Structure.** The live log file holds two regions:
1. **Recent (< 30 days):** full, verbose entries.
2. **Archive (>= 30 days):** compacted rows in an abbreviated encoding, preceded by
   a **legend and expansion instruction block** — the abbreviation table plus prose
   telling a reader how to reconstitute a full entry from a compacted row.

The expansion contract lives *in the same file as the data*. An archive that needs an
external decoder is one refactor away from being unreadable.

**What to record.** Command, target project, resolved engine, duration, and outcome
per stage: zombies found/terminated, bytecode files removed, caches purged, cache
removal failures, registry entries pruned, Ruff/Pyright pass/fail and durations,
whether `--smart` was used and how many files it scoped.

**Why this is worth having.** Every finding in the 2026-08-30 omni audit —
`omni clean` removing nothing while reporting success, a `wmic` call that had been a
silent no-op since Windows deprecated it, a PID registry grown to 48 entries over two
months — would have been *visible in a trend* rather than requiring a from-scratch
forensic pass. A tool that governs every build should be able to say what it has been
doing.

**Design cautions:**
- Compaction must be **idempotent and crash-safe**. It rewrites the operator's only
  record of tool history; a partial rewrite is data loss. Write-temp-then-atomic-swap,
  as `concurrency.atomic_write_text` already does for MACCRE artifacts.
- The abbreviation table must be **versioned**. A compacted row encoded under v1 and
  read under v2 legend silently misreports.
- Do not compact `build_pipeline.log` in place — that file is protected from
  `omni clean` precisely because it is the existing record. Introduce a separate
  telemetry file rather than mutating it.

**Cross-reference:** overlaps the proposed `omni doctor` (see
`C:\OmniBuilder\omni-proposed-improvements.txt`, NEW ITEM E) — `doctor` reports
present state, this records state over time. `doctor` is the natural producer of the
per-run telemetry record.

***

### Feature Name: `omni` hardening — deferred items carrying real risk
**Abstract:** Three `omni clean` improvements were audited and deliberately not applied because they terminate processes or can destroy data; they want a dry-run mode first.
**Date/Time Entered:** 2026-08-30T00:00:00-04:00
**Status:** Deferred (needs prerequisite — `omni clean --dry-run`)
**Verified:** Reproduced and documented in full at `.oracle_artifacts/2026-08-30_omni_clean_hardening_audit.md` and `C:\OmniBuilder\omni-proposed-improvements.txt`.

**Description:**
Implemented on 2026-08-30: recursive bytecode purge (the previous one was a no-op
by construction), bounded retry with honest failure reporting, `Get-CimInstance`
replacing the dead `wmic` call, dead-PID registry pruning, and the `--smart` flag
that had been declared and ignored.

**Deferred, with reasons:**

1. **Broaden the zombie match pattern.** It matches command lines containing
   `swarm_worker`, which was right when each worker was a process. Since Phase 6.12
   workers are *threads* inside one interpreter, so an orphan is a whole `run.py`/TUI
   process. Broadening is a change in **policy**, and it can terminate the operator's
   own session.
2. **Corroborate before terminating from `.session_pids.json`.** PIDs are recycled.
   Of three surviving PIDs in MACCRE's own registry, one had become an editor
   terminal, one `PresentationFontCache.exe`, one `LogiPluginService.exe`. An entry is
   actionable only when PID **and** creation time **and** command line all agree.
   Until then, killing from that file is forbidden — it prunes only.
3. **WAL/SHM cleanup beyond the root.** MACCRE databases live under
   `__DATACENTER/<project>/`, so `swarm_queue.db-wal` is never reached. **Do not
   simply switch to `rglob`** — a `-wal` left by a crashed process can hold committed
   transactions never checkpointed, and deleting it loses them. Correct sequence:
   hunt zombies, then `PRAGMA wal_checkpoint(TRUNCATE)` per database, then remove only
   what remains after a clean open.

All three want **`omni clean --dry-run`** first — enumerate and report, change
nothing.

***

### Feature Name: Omniscience — spatial system interface for omni
**Abstract:** An omni GUI applying what was learned building RadonVec: maps the system spatially, lets the operator send commands at targets in space, and lets new components be scoped spatially while coding. Standalone or MACCRE-compatible, in the manner of Sovereign Importer.
**Date/Time Entered:** 2026-08-30T00:00:00-04:00
**Status:** Deferred (needs prerequisite — the File Cabinet read API)
**Verified:** N/A — new capability. Full feasibility assessment at `C:\OmniBuilder\omni-proposed-improvements.txt`, NEW ITEM F.

**Description:**
Assessed feasible and worth doing, **but not as one thing.** Summary of the
assessment; the full version with reasoning lives in the omni proposals file.

**RadonVec's renderer and RadonVec's mathematics belong to different panes.** Its
tomographic pipeline (PCA → voxel → Radon → FBP) recovers *density peaks*, not
labels, and works because embedding clouds are sparse filamentary graphs. A MACCRE
topology is a small, discrete, **labelled** DAG already held exactly. Pushing it
through that pipeline would lossily compress perfect data — and then "send commands
at targets" would have to *infer* which node a recovered peak was. Approximate
identity driving an action is the exact failure that deadlocked an 8-lane gather on
2026-08-30: a *wrong* non-empty identifier is worse than none.

Its mathematics **is** right for a telemetry pane — index drift, cluster collapse,
fragmentation over time, with the needle-on-a-groove time scrubber. So: two panes
sharing a renderer and a camera, **not** a data model. RadonVec's interactive
Three.js visualizer is the genuinely reusable asset.

**One inversion worth noting:** the omni proposals doc rightly says do not build an
AST dependency graph *for QA gating*. For a **map** the risk inverts — a wrong edge
shown to a human is visible and checkable; a wrong edge in test-selection silently
skips a test. A stdlib `ast` import scan is sufficient here and needs no dependency.

**Prerequisite already met:** `maccre_core/orchestration/topology_graph.describe()`
(2026-08-30) returns nodes, edges, entry nodes, terminal nodes and unreachable nodes
in one call, built deliberately as the single seam so a renderer reads *the graph the
engine executes* rather than a lookalike.

**Prerequisite still needed:** a stable read API over the datacenter — the same thing
Sovereign Importer needs. Omniscience should not precede that work, or it will invent
a second divergent read path.

**Recommended sequence:** `omni doctor` → File Cabinet read API → structural pane
(read-only) → addressing and command dispatch → RadonVec volumetric telemetry pane →
spatial authoring last.

***

### Feature Name: Node-ID convention divergence between TUI and engine
**Abstract:** The TUI visualiser builds node ids with a `_{i}` suffix while the engine hydrates with `_S{i}`; two representations of one graph that are already drifting.
**Date/Time Entered:** 2026-08-30T00:00:00-04:00
**Status:** Unfulfilled
**Verified:** Read directly — `maccre_tui/nexus_plex.py` uses `f"{a}_{i}"`; `flow_engine._hydrate_topology` uses `f"{node_id}_S{step_index}"`.

**Description:**
Currently cosmetic, because the visualiser only draws. It stops being cosmetic the
moment anything *acts* on the rendered graph — a spatial map, a telemetry sweep, or
click-to-configure — because the layer would be addressing nodes that do not exist in
the queue.

Fix direction: have the TUI read the engine's graph through
`topology_graph.describe()` rather than constructing its own node ids. That module
exists for exactly this reason.

**Related:** *Omniscience* above, and Phase 6.13 Task B5 (multi-lane rendering).

### Feature Name: Scatter lane authoring ownership must be settled before two surfaces ship
**Abstract:** "Scope new system components spatially" (Omniscience) and Phase 6.13 §6.14 multi-lane authoring both build authoring UI over the same topology graph; two authoring surfaces will diverge as the node-id conventions already have.
**Date/Time Entered:** 2026-08-30T00:00:00-04:00
**Status:** Deferred (needs decision)
**Verified:** N/A — architectural conflict, identified during the Omniscience assessment.

**Description:**
Decide which surface owns topology authoring before either ships. The precedent is
immediately above: two independent representations of one graph drifted within a
single codebase. Two independent *authoring* paths would drift the same way, with
writes rather than reads at stake.
